# Retro Rack: full context for an AI assistant

Generated by `tools/build.js` from game version **fc971ef2**. Do not edit by hand: change the files it is made from and rebuild.

This one file holds what an AI needs to understand and change Retro Rack: the project notes, a map of the code, and the full source of everything written for the project. Left out: the three.js engine and the pixel fonts in `vendor/` (third-party, unchanged), the built `index.html` (generated from `src/`), images, and the browser test scripts (listed in the map).

Live game: https://flopp-r.github.io/retro-rack/ · Repository: https://github.com/flopp-r/retro-rack

Read AGENTS.md first: it explains how the game works and the rules any change must keep (above all, bit-identical physics in both browsers for online play).

# File: AGENTS.md

# Retro Rack: guide for AI assistants and developers

This is the main guide for anyone, person or AI, who changes this project. Claude Code reads it through `CLAUDE.md`; other AI coding tools (OpenAI Codex, Cursor, GitHub Copilot and others) read `AGENTS.md` directly. A chat AI that can't open the repository can be given `CONTEXT.md` instead: this guide, the other notes, a map of the code and the full source in one file (`CONTEXT-SHORT.md` is the same without the source).

## Start here

**What it is.** Retro Rack is a 3D pixel-art pool game: American 8-ball and 9-ball on a 9 ft table, British reds & yellows on a 7 ft table, practice with trick shots, a CPU opponent with four skill levels, a career mode (a tour of knockout tournaments against named computer players, from the pub to the national tour), a locker of looks (cloths, cues and floating gloves, bought or won in cases), same-device play, and online 1v1 through a small Cloudflare relay. It is one self-contained web page, hosted on GitHub Pages from this repository, and installable as an app. Live: https://flopp-r.github.io/retro-rack/

**Read in this order**
1. This file: how it works, the rules, and how to test and ship a change.
2. `HANDOVER.md`: why things are the way they are, reference numbers (physics, tables, lighting) and a dated list of every change shipped.
3. `README.md`: how the owner set it up, and what players see.
4. The code: `src/core.js` (physics, rules, CPU), `src/career.js` (the career tour), `src/looks.js` (cases and the locker), then `src/game.js` (everything in the browser). Both are split into sections marked `// ------ name`; the map in `CONTEXT.md` lists them with line numbers.

**Rules that must never be broken**
1. **Identical physics in both browsers.** Online, each browser simulates every shot and the results must match to the last bit. See "Determinism" below before touching anything `step()` calls.
2. **`index.html` is built output.** Edit `src/`, then run `node tools/build.js`, and commit both.
3. **A clean console:** no errors, warnings or deprecated APIs.
4. **`main` is live.** Pushing to `main` deploys the site (and the relay, if `relay/` changed). Work on a branch and test first.
5. **Online changes need both players on the same version.** Ask the owner before changing online messages or behaviour, and keep old fields working where possible.
6. **The repository is public.** Never commit personal details, passwords, tokens or keys. The relay address in `config.js` is fine; it has to be public.

## Project conventions

- Explain changes in plain language, say what will change on screen, and keep jargon out of summaries.
- UK English and metric units everywhere: in the UI, comments and messages.
- Ask before large redesigns, as well as before anything that changes online play (rule 5 above).
- Every menu screen must fit a phone held sideways (762×341) without scrolling. The locker's inventory and shop are long lists that scroll inside the panel (`.lockList`); the screen itself still fits.
- Code style: match the file you're in. Compact, mostly one statement per idea, comments only where the reason isn't obvious. No build step beyond `tools/build.js`, and no frameworks or npm packages in the game.

## Files

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `AGENTS.md` | This guide. `CLAUDE.md` just points Claude Code here. |
| `HANDOVER.md` | Decisions and why, reference numbers, the ideas list, and the list of changes shipped. |
| `README.md` | Setting up your own copy, playing online, installing the app, features. |
| `CONTEXT.md`, `CONTEXT-SHORT.md` | **Generated** by the build: the context packs for AI assistants (see top). Never edit by hand. |
| `index.html` | **Built output.** Never edit by hand; edit `src/` and run the build. It is committed because GitHub Pages serves it. |
| `config.js` | The relay address (`window.RETRO_RACK_RELAY`). Leave it alone unless asked; it holds the live relay URL. |
| `sw.js` | The service worker for the installable app. Network first: the saved copy is only used offline. |
| `manifest.webmanifest`, `icons/` | The app's name, colours and icons. The icons are generated by `tools/make-icons.js`. |
| `src/core.js` | Physics, tables, rules and the CPU player. No DOM; also runs in Node for tests and tools. |
| `src/career.js` | The career: tiers, events, opponents (with their CPU personalities and measured ratings), draws, results, prize money and the saved career. No DOM; also runs in Node. |
| `src/looks.js` | Looks: the cloths, cues and gloves that come in cases, the case grades (odds, prices, the guarantee), earning, and the locker (money, unopened cases, what's owned). No DOM; also runs in Node. |
| `src/game.js` | Everything in the browser: rendering, camera, input, HUD, menus, sound, replay, trick shots, career screens and matches, the locker screen and gloves, online play. |
| `src/shell.html` | Page markup and CSS, with placeholders the build fills in (fonts, `__BUNDLE__`). |
| `vendor/` | three.js r159 ES-module build and the two pixel fonts (with their licences). |
| `tools/build.js` | Builds `index.html`: inlines fonts, three.js and the game code into one self-contained file. |
| `tools/make-context.js` | Makes `CONTEXT.md` and `CONTEXT-SHORT.md`; the build runs it. |
| `tools/find-trick-demos.js` | Searches with the real physics for "Show me" demo shots for trick-shot layouts. |
| `tools/sim-career.js` | Measures each career opponent's strength by playing frames against the Medium CPU with the real physics. |
| `tests/physics.test.js` | Physics, determinism, pocket and rules tests, plus a check that the context packs are up to date. |
| `tests/career.test.js` | Career tests: tour data, draws, results and money, saving and loading, opponents' CPU settings. |
| `tests/looks.test.js` | Looks tests: the catalogue, case odds and the guarantee, opening, buying, duplicates, earning, the saved locker. |
| `tests/browser/` | Browser tests: real pages in headless Chromium against a local relay. `run-all.js` runs them; see Testing. |
| `package.json` | npm shortcuts for the commands below (`npm run build`, `npm test`, `npm run test:browser`). Nothing to install. |
| `relay/` | The Cloudflare Worker (Durable Objects) used for online rooms, the open-rooms list and the ideas players send. |
| `.nojekyll` | Stops GitHub Pages running Jekyll over the repository. |

## Commands

No installs are needed for the game itself, only Node.

- `node tools/build.js`: rebuild `index.html` after any change in `src/`. It also remakes `CONTEXT.md` and `CONTEXT-SHORT.md`, so run it after changing the notes too.
- `node tests/physics.test.js`, `node tests/career.test.js` and `node tests/looks.test.js`: the physics, rules, career and looks tests (`npm test` runs all three). All must pass before pushing.
- `node tools/sim-career.js [names] [frames]`: measure career opponents' strength in their tier's game (all of them, 60 frames each, by default; the 36 opponents at 80 frames take about 40 minutes on 4 cores). `@hard:9ball` and the like measure a CPU level as a yardstick. Put the ratings it prints into `src/career.js`.
- `node tests/browser/run-all.js [words]`: run the browser tests (all of them, or only files whose names contain the words). See Testing.
- `node tools/find-trick-demos.js`: check the trick-shot demos still work, and find new ones.
- `node tools/make-icons.js`: redraw the app icons (pixel art, no installs). The build inlines `icons/favicon-32.png`.
- Relay, locally: `cd relay && npm install && npx wrangler dev`. Then open `index.html?relay=ws://127.0.0.1:8787` in two separate browser windows to play yourself online.

## Deployment (all automatic on push to `main`)

- **GitHub Pages** serves the repository root (`index.html`, `config.js`) at https://flopp-r.github.io/retro-rack/ within a minute or two. Browsers cache; tell the owner to press Ctrl+F5 after an update.
- **Cloudflare Workers Builds** watches the same repository with root directory `relay`, and runs `npx wrangler deploy` when anything changes. The Worker must stay named `retro-rack-relay`, matching `relay/wrangler.jsonc`.
- Both run on free plans. Keep relay changes cheap: the WebSocket Hibernation API, no polling, no per-message storage writes.

## How changes are made

1. Agree the change with the owner first: explain the trade-offs, recommend one, then build what they pick.
2. Work on a branch. Edit `src/` (and the notes), run `node tools/build.js`, run the tests (see Testing), and commit `src/`, `index.html` and the context packs together.
3. Open a pull request into `main`. When the owner says to merge, squash-merge it.
4. GitHub Pages publishes within a minute or two; the "pages build and deployment" run in the repository's Actions confirms it. Read the version from the committed `index.html` (`const BUILD = '…'`), not from memory, and tell the owner, who checks it at the bottom of the menu after pressing Ctrl+F5.
5. On pull requests, the Cloudflare check "Workers Builds: retro-rack-relay" fails at once: it's a preview build, and is unrelated unless `relay/` changed. The production relay deploys from `main` as normal.

## Architecture

### Physics (`src/core.js`)
- Units are metres, kilograms and seconds. The time step is 1 ms.
- Balls carry position, velocity and a full 3D angular velocity. The model covers:
  - sliding versus rolling friction against the cloth, and spin decay about the vertical axis;
  - ball-to-ball collisions with speed-dependent friction, which produces throw;
  - cushions whose nose sits above the ball's centre, so side spin changes the rebound angle;
  - squirt from side spin.
- Collisions rewind to the exact moment of contact before resolving.
- Tables are data in `SPECS` (`us9`, `uk7`). Call `setTable(key)` to switch. Pockets have angled jaws, rounded (filleted) on the UK table.

### Determinism: critical for online play
Both browsers simulate every shot and must get bit-identical results, so keep these rules:
- Inside `step()` and the functions it calls, use only `+ - * /`, `Math.sqrt`, `abs`, `min`, `max`, `round` and `sign`. Engines may differ in the last bit for `Math.exp`, `sin`, `cos`, `pow`, `atan2` and similar. Use `detExp()` for exponentials.
- Table geometry is snapped to 1e-9 m with `q9()`, so trig differences during setup vanish.
- `strike()` does use trig, so the shooter sends the resulting velocities and the receiver checks them against its own calculation.
- No `Math.random` in the physics. Racks are generated by the host and sent as exact numbers.
- The tests check determinism; keep that test passing.

### Rules
`judge(game, rec, before, after, player)` decides fouls and wins from what happened during a shot (`rec`: first ball hit, cushions hit, balls potted). `nextGame()` applies the result. Modes:
- `8ball`: WPA-style, without called pockets.
- `9ball`.
- `uk8`: British pub rules. Two visits after a foul, a free ball on the first visit, ball in hand behind the baulk line after an in-off. Optional `game.oneVisitOnBlack`: a player on the black only ever gets one visit; `nextGame()` needs the table after the shot (its fourth argument) to apply it.
- `practice`.

The CPU (`planBot`) is a generator, so its search can be spread across frames. It simulates candidate shots with the same physics and scores the outcomes. Its strength levels are in `DIFF` (each field is explained above it); `planBot` also takes a profile object with the same fields, which is how career opponents get their personalities (`long` adds aim error on long pots).

### Career (`src/career.js`, and the career section of `game.js`)
- **The tour:** `TIERS` hold events: the pub circuit (reds & yellows, 7 ft), the club and hall circuits (8-ball, 9 ft) and the national tour (9-ball, 9 ft), three events each, every event with a `sign` for the neon sign. Each event is an 8-player knockout (you and 7 of `field`), with `races` (frames to win the quarter-final, semi-final and final) and `prize` (money for going out in each round, then for winning). Winning an event opens the next (`unlocked()`); winning the tier's last event completes the tier.
- **Opponents:** `OPPONENTS` gives each a name, a pixel portrait (`look`, drawn by `portrait()` in `game.js` from the `SPRITES` styles), a blurb, a base CPU level plus overrides (`cfg`), optional `nerves` (execution error multiplier on the final ball, see `onFinalBall()`), and a `rating` measured with `tools/sim-career.js` (Medium CPU = 1500). Ratings settle computer-v-computer matches in a draw (`simMatch()`, Elo-style, always within one game) and, through `strength()`, give the strength stars (9-ball ratings are stretched to compare with 8-ball, using the Hard CPU as the yardstick; see `GAME_SCALE`).
- **Draws and results** are seeded (`rng()`), so reloading never changes them. `enterEvent()` makes the draw (top two seeds in opposite halves), `recordMatch()` records your match, plays out the rest of the round, pays prizes and finishes the event; `withdraw()` counts as losing the current match. `validate()` checks a saved or imported career and returns a clean copy.
- **Money and the shop:** the career only counts what it has won (`earned`); the prize money itself goes into the device's locker (see Looks), by `careerPaid()` in `game.js`, with the case an event win brings. `SHOP` lists the cloths and cues sold outright (cue designs colour the shaft, joint, forearm, wrap, butt and inlay; `HOUSE_CUE` is the free one). Saves from before the locker kept `money` and `bought`; `validate()` keeps them and `game.js` moves them into the locker once.
- **In the browser:** the career lives in `CAR.data` (localStorage `retroRack.career`). Screens `cnew` (new career: name, look, aim guide fixed for the career), `career` (the hub: one tier at a time, chosen with tabs; `careerTier()` picks the one to show; its Locker button opens the locker) and `cevent` (the draw and your next opponent), drawn by `careerMenus()`; the footer's `#bCareer` and `#bWithdraw` buttons act on them. Save to file / Load from file export and import the career as JSON, with the locker inside (`locker`); loading merges it in without taking anything away.
- **Venues:** `VENUES` in `game.js` dress the room (carpet, walls, skirting, the neon sign's colours) for each tier; `setVenue(key, sign)` swaps them. A career match, and the event screen behind the menu, use the event's tier and sign; everything else uses `home`.
- **Cues:** the cue's parts take a design's colours (`applyCue()`), and a pattern drawn round the forearm (`cuePattern()`) with its effect (`cueFx()`); `cueFor(player)` decides whose cue is at the table: yours, a career opponent's own (`cue` in `OPPONENTS`), the house cue for the CPU, and online the other player's. Gloves follow the same rules (`gloveFor()`, `glove` in `OPPONENTS`; the CPU otherwise has none).

### Looks and the locker (`src/looks.js`, and the locker and gloves sections of `game.js`)
- **The catalogue:** `CASE_ITEMS` holds 60 looks that come only in the everyday cases (and see Seasons for Halloween's 30): 14 patterned cloths (`pat`, drawn by `drawPattern()` onto the bed as a texture, metre for metre), 20 cues (`pat` and `pc` for a forearm pattern, `fx` glow, neon or rainbow) and 26 gloves (`style`, one of the glove types in `STYLE` in `game.js`: sport, driver, tactical, moto, fingerless and hand wraps, plus three novelties, boxing, robot claw and skeleton; and `fx` glow, flicker or ghost). Each has a rarity (`RARITY`: common, rare, epic, legendary and mythic, with the price a duplicate sells for). The legendary and mythic ones are painted pixel by pixel from noise (`makeNoise()`, `fbm()` and `paint()`, in the collection effects section): the cloths by `clothGalaxy()`, `clothSynth()`, `clothAurora()` and `clothMagma()` (at half the bed's usual resolution, and the last four kept in `CLOTH_CACHE`, so they're quick), the cues by `cuePaint()` (`pat` holo, tubes, swirl and plasma), the gloves by `gloveTex()` (gold leaf, fire, smoke, molten crust and holographic bands, wrapped round each finger and laid across the hand; only legendary and mythic gloves have one).
- **Mythics** (red, two of each kind) move: the cloths Aurora (`anim: 'flow'`, the texture drifts, `clothFx()`) and Magma (`anim: 'pulse'`, its cracks glow through an emissive map, `clothGlow`), the cues Event horizon (`fx: 'spin'`) and Plasma (`fx: 'plasma'`), whose seamless forearm patterns scroll in `cueFx()`, and the gloves Molten and Prism (`fx: 'molten'`, `'prism'`), whose glow (Molten) or bands (Prism) move in `gloveFx()`. Brightness only changes slowly (nothing faster than about 3 times a second), and with reduced motion they hold still. `ALL` is every look by id: the shop's, the cases' and `FREE_GLOVE`. `FREE` lists what everyone owns ('house', 'none', the free glove; the five original cloths are free too).
- **Cases:** `GRADES` (bronze, silver, gold, diamond) give the odds of each rarity, the cost to open one and the price to buy one; legendaries only come in silver and better; mythics come in every grade, very rarely (0.2% in bronze up to 6% in diamond). The diamond case holds only epic or better (70% epic, 24% legendary, 6% mythic) and costs the most to open (£250) and buy (£900); its card says "Always epic or better" (`caseCard()` writes that for any grade whose odds start at epic). `roll()` picks the prize (an epic or better is guaranteed within `PITY` = 8 cases), `openCase()` pays, picks and sells a duplicate at once (but a mythic is always one not owned yet, until all six are), `reelItems()` fills the reel with items drawn from the same odds. `buyCase()` and `buyLook()` spend money.
- **Earning:** `cpuFrameWon()` (a frame won against the computer pays `CPU_PAY`, £15 on Easy up to £150 on Expert, and moves the level's meter on; `METER` = 1, so every frame won earns a case of the level's grade), `careerFrameWon()` (a case of the tier's grade for every career frame won, no pay), `eventWon()` (`EVENT_CASES` = 2 cases of the tier's grade, a championship two of the grade above, four of the best at the top) and `onlineWon()` (the day's first online win, once per local date: £100 and two gold cases). Practice and same-device games earn nothing. `frameEarn()` in `game.js` calls them when a frame ends; `EARN` holds the result for the result screen.
- **Seasons** (`SERIES`; Halloween, 1 October to 10 November): looks that come only in the season's own cases (`SEASON_ITEMS`, `series: 'halloween'`, 30 of them, two of each kind at each rarity; `SERIES_POOL`, `seriesItems()`), apart from the everyday cases and the 15 collections. `GRADE_IDS` are the everyday cases; `CASE_IDS` adds the seasons' (`trick` and `treat`, `series` on the grade; `poolOf()` gives a case's looks). Trick: earned and bought, any of its looks, duplicates sold. Treat (`fresh`): bought only, free to open, and `roll()` leaves out what you own until you own them all. `inSeason(sid, day)` says whether a local date (YYYY-MM-DD) is in it: `buyCase(l, grade, day)` refuses a season's case out of season ('season') and a Treat case once the set is complete ('complete'); `seasonWon(l, day)` gives the season's daily case for the first frame won each day (`sdaily`), called by `frameEarn()` for any frame that earns (EARN.season). Each rarity's six looks pay `SERIES.halloween.reward[r]` once (claimed `'halloween:rarity'`), all thirty `grand` (`'halloween:all'`): £5,000 and the `haunted` effect. Held season cases open at any time, and nothing is ever taken away.
- **Collections:** the case looks of one rarity and one kind make a collection (`COLLECTIONS`, keyed `'rarity:kind'`, 15 in all; `colItems()` lists one's looks): the common cloths, the mythic gloves and so on. Each pays money and cases when complete (`KIND_REWARD`: common £150 and a silver case, up to mythic £5,000 and three diamond cases). All three of a rarity make its set (`SETS`), which pays far more and adds the rarity's effect (`EFFECTS`): common £1,000, three gold cases and the chalk trail; rare £2,500, three diamond cases, gold rails; epic £5,000, five diamond cases, pot sparks; legendary £12,000, eight diamond cases, your name in neon; mythic £30,000, twelve diamond cases, the cosmic cue ball. All fifteen (`GRAND`): £50,000 and the starfield room. `claimCollections()` pays whatever is due, each only once (collections, then sets, then 'all'); `openCase()` calls it and returns what it completed as `done`, and `claimDue()` in `game.js` calls it after loading a career file and at start-up. `effects()` lists the effects earned. Dev mode's test locker counts every reward as already paid.
- **The locker** (localStorage `retroRack.locker`, `LOCK` in `game.js`): `money`, `owned`, `cases`, `meter`, `pity`, `daily`, `opened`, `claimed` (the collections paid); `validateLocker()` cleans a saved one and `mergeLocker()` brings in one from a career file. Every change goes through `lockerEdit()`, which reads the stored copy, changes it and writes it straight back, so two open pages don't undo each other's changes; a `storage` listener shows changes made in another page. A new locker starts with £40 and a silver case, so everyone can try one. On first load the locker takes in looks from older versions (`S.owned`) and a career's unspent money.
- **The locker screen** (`locker`, from the home screen's Locker card or the career hub), drawn by `renderLocker()`, with three tabs:
  - **Cases:** each grade's odds, Open and Buy, the meters and the guarantee.
  - **Inventory:** one long list (`invRow()`) of everything owned, rarest first (`lookRank()`, `lookSort()`: mythic down to common, then the shop's looks, then the free ones), under a heading per rarity; tapping one puts it in use. Above it, the collection rewards earned, each with an On/Off switch (`S.fxOff`).
  - **Halloween** (`'hw'`, shown in the season, whenever a Halloween case or look is held, and in dev mode; `seriesShown()`; on phones the tab is a pumpkin icon, so the four tabs keep to one row): the season's dates, the daily case, the Trick and Treat cards (`caseCard()`, shared with the Cases tab; Buy is dimmed out of season and says when it's on), then each rarity's six looks with its reward, rarest first, and the all-thirty reward. `seasonDates()` writes the dates out.
  - **Shop:** the looks for sale outright in a first row, then a section per rarity, rarest first: its set (how many of its three collections are done, and the set's reward), then its cloth, cue and glove collections, each with how many you have, its reward and its looks (`colTile()`: owned tiles bright with a tick, the rest dimmed); a last line for all fifteen.
  - `lookTap()` handles a tap anywhere: an owned look goes into use; one you don't own (or a shop look you don't buy) is marked to try on (`LOCKV.try`, by kind, dashed outline). The lists keep their scroll position when they redraw.
  - Item pictures are pixel art: `clothImg()`, `cueIcon()`, `gloveImg()`, `caseImg()`. Settings has a picker for each kind (`lookPicker()`).
- **The money counter** (`#bMoney`, `purse()` and `PURSE`, in the locker section of `game.js`): the locker's money with a pixel pound coin, on every menu screen, in the top-right corner beside the Settings cog. `refreshMenus()` calls `purse()`, which counts to the new amount (500 ms for a small change up to 1.4 s for a big one) with the change drifting in under it (`.moneyDelta.up`, green) or falling away (`.down`, rose). While the menu is hidden (a game, Preview, the waiting room) it leaves the shown amount alone, so money earned in a game is counted in on the way back. Opening a case sets `PURSE.hold` to what's left after paying, and `reelDone()` clears it, so the counter shows the cost at once but a duplicate's sale or a collection's reward only when the reel stops. Tapping it opens the locker (not during the reel, or from the locker). In dev mode it has a dashed border and says Test locker. With reduced motion the amount just changes. Display only.
- **Preview** (`#bPreview` in the footer of the inventory and the shop, `startPreview()`, `endPreview()`): the menu steps aside and the camera (`cam.mode` `'show'`) swings slowly round the side of the cue, lined up on the nearest ball, showing your cloth, cue and gloves with any look marked to try in its place (`PREV.looks`; `cueFor()`, `gloveFor()` and `setCloth()` use them while `PREV.on`). Nothing is saved. A tap anywhere (`#preview`), Esc or the back gesture ends it; with reduced motion the camera holds still.
- **The reel** (`#reel`, `showReel()`): the locker pays and picks the prize first, then a strip of the case's items glides past a marker and stops on it; the other items come from the case's odds and the stopping point within the prize's tile is random, so it never fakes a near miss. Each tile is in its rarity's colour (`reelTile()`), fading from a dark shade at the top to nearly the full colour at the bottom. It takes 7 seconds to stop, slowing to a long crawl. Tapping the reel skips to the end; with reduced motion there's no spin. A case that completes a collection, a set or all fifteen says so under the prize (`doneTxt()`). The reel fills most of the screen (tiles 40% of its height, at most 22% of its width) over a dim that leaves the room barely visible.
- **The Haunted Hall** (the room section of `game.js`, `HALL`): the `haunted` effect, at home only, winning over the starfield room. `dressRoom()` (called by `setVenue()` and `applyFx()`) sets the floor, walls and skirting for the venue and calls `showHall()`; `hallTex()` paints its textures once (damask, boards, cobweb, the windows' night skies, cloud, fog, the portrait, a jack-o'-lantern's carving) and `buildHall()` makes it the first time it's shown: beams along the walls with bats hanging from them, twelve ribbed jack-o'-lanterns carved on the side facing the room (their glow is an emissive map) with three orange lights, two arched windows on the far wall, cobwebs in the top corners, by the table's legs and on the lamp, three layers of floor fog, the crooked portrait on the left wall and a skeleton slumped in the far corner. Everything stays by the walls and under the table, out of every camera's view of the play. `hallFx()` each frame: candlelight flickering slowly (no faster than about once a second), a bat now and then stirring its wings, cloud and fog drifting, the portrait's pupils turned towards the cue ball, and the cobwebs kept by the table's legs and lamp. With reduced motion it all holds still. The hemisphere light dims and turns violet while it's up (`HEMI`), the sign reads Haunted Hall in orange and purple (`HALL.sign`, unless your name is in neon). Display only, on your own screen.
- **Collection effects** (the collection effects section of `game.js`): `fxOn(k)` is true when the reward is earned and not switched off; `applyFx()` puts them in place after any change. The chalk trail (`TRAIL`, an instanced mesh of dots laid every 1.2 cm along the cue ball's path, shrinking away), gold rails (`railMat` takes `goldTex()` instead of the wood), pot sparks (`sparkAt()`, from the pocket in the ball's colour; off with reduced motion), your name on the home neon sign (`signText()`: the career's name, else the online name), the cosmic cue ball (`cosmicTex()`, pale and bright, with gold spin dots) and the starfield room (`starTex()` on the walls at home). `tableFx()` moves the trail and sparks each frame. Display only, and only on your own screen: nothing is sent online.
- **The show behind the reel** (`#reelFx`, `fxStart()`, `fxFrame()`, drawn at a quarter of the screen's resolution and scaled up): faint stripes while it spins; when it stops, stripes in the prize's rarity colour and its own colour bounce round the screen, more and faster the rarer it is, with sparkles from epic, turning rays from legendary, and for a mythic shifting colours, rings, confetti, a shake and a bigger fanfare (`sfx('fanfare', tier)`). It stops when the reel closes; with reduced motion it's drawn once, still.
- **Gloves** (the gloves section of `game.js`): the one-piece hands are the owner's own procedural model, "Retro Rack hands", built in: low-poly and flat-shaded (the materials use `flatShading`; the geometry has no shared corners), one mesh per hand on a skeleton of 17 bones (`GH_BONES`: the arm, the wrist, three for each finger and three for the thumb). In the model's frame the fingers point along -z and the back of the hand faces +y; `GH_FINGERS` and `GH_THUMB` give each knuckle, width and section lengths for a right hand, and a left hand is a true mirror (`ghMake()` flips x and turns each face back round), so the poses work on both. `ghTris()` lofts rings of corners (`ghRing()`, `ghLoft()`, small boxes from `ghBlock()`) into the cuff (darker inside), the wrist and palm, four six-sided fingers and the thumb, then the type's extras from `STYLE`: a wrist strap, a back panel, a knuckle strip or a raised guard, finger pads, nails where the fingers are bare. Each triangle has a part (slot) that `ghMat()` turns into one of the glove's eight materials (`GLOVE_KEYS`: the back in the main colour, the palm side a little lighter, skin past a fingerless glove's first joint and on all of a wrap's fingers, trims toned towards the main colour by `TONE`); a textured glove's picture covers the back, palm, panel and trims alike. `ghGeo()` makes each type's mesh once per side (`HAND_GEO`, 700 to 770 triangles in four to six groups, one per material used); `ghHand()` puts one on its skeleton in a pose from `GH_POSES` (grip, bridge, and the reactions fist, thumb and open). A pose with an `anchor` says where the cue's middle line passes through the hand, and `ghAttach()` places the hand so the cue runs there: the grip on the cue's butt (a child of the cue, so it strokes with it, thumb towards the tip), and the bridge on the cloth behind the cue ball, kept flat (`level`), its anchor `restHeight` above the cloth; when the cue's line is higher than that (top spin), `updateGloves()` tips the hand up on its fingertips rather than lifting it off the cloth. The boxing glove is one faceted mitt (`boxingGeo()`, the cue resting on its thumb beside it); the robot claw and the skeleton keep their jointed parts (`partsHand()`, with `FINGER`, `THUMB` and `HAND_POSE`, in an older frame with the fingers along +x, which `handBuild()` also turns the one-piece hands into for the reactions). `gloveBuild()` makes the grip, the bridge and the reaction hand. `cheer()` plays a reaction after a shot (a thumbs-up for a pot, a shrug for a foul, a fist pump for winning the frame), off with reduced motion and during replays. A fingertip of the bridge drums while you line up. The cue hand hides when the camera is within 0.3 m of it. Display only: nothing here affects the physics.
- **Career matches** are normal CPU games: `careerPlay()` puts the menu's own choices (`M`) aside in `CAR.stash`, sets `M` from the event, and calls `startGame(false, false, resume)`, where `resume` carries the match score, who breaks and a frame in progress. `CAR.on` is true for the match: `saveM()` and Play again leave the menu's choices alone, the scoreboard and messages use the opponent's name (`botName()`), and Pause has no aim guide choice and no re-rack. `careerSave()` stores the table (`snapshot()`) after every shot, so a match resumes exactly; `careerFrameOver()` records a finished frame and, when the match is won or lost, `recordMatch()`. `careerLeave()` (from `toMenu()`) puts `M` back.

### Browser (`src/game.js`)
- **Rendering:** the scene is drawn at low resolution into a render target. A post-processing shader then adds depth-based outlines, a vignette, Bayer dithering and colour quantisation. Lights use three.js physical units; the legacy lighting mode is gone.
- **States:** `menu`, `lobby`, `aim`, `set` (Shoot pressed, the aim held while the shot camera swings in), `stroke`, `moving`, `remote` (waiting for the online opponent), `botThink`, `botAim` and `over`.
- **Replay:** stores the table before the last shot plus the cue ball's initial velocities, then re-simulates.
- **Trick shots:** the `TRICKS` list sits near the top of `game.js`. Saved layouts are kept in localStorage. In "Edit table" mode (`EDIT`), "Add balls" opens a picker (`renderBallPick()`, `toggleBall()`) to put balls on the table or take them off; it closes when Pause opens.
- **Settings:** `S` in localStorage (`retroRack.settings`). The panel (`#pause`: Pause in a game, the cog in the menus) has tabs along the top (`#pTabs`, `.pTab`, `data-tab`), each its own page that scrolls (`.pPage`, `#pg…`): **Match** (in a game only: what's being played, `#pSub`, and big Resume, Re-rack or Reset table, Offer re-rack, Concede frame and Quit to menu buttons, `#pauseGame`), **Play** (aim guide, markers, shot camera, follow next shot), **Looks** (cloth, cue, gloves), **Display** (pixel size, colours, dither, outlines, scanlines), **Sound** (volume, vibration) and **Dev** (dev mode only, `#tabDev`). Resume (Done in the menus, `#bResume`) sits at the end of the tab row. `togglePause()` opens it on Match in a game and on the last settings tab used from the menus (`PT`, not saved), each page scrolled to the top; `pauseTab(name, slide)` shows a tab: the highlight (`#pInk`) glides under it (`inkTo()`, which also scrolls the tab row to it on a narrow screen), and its page slides in from that side (`.slide`, `--dx`), over 0.22 s; ← and → move between the tabs. Opening, the panel fades and grows slightly. With reduced motion the CSS leaves all the movement out. A large panel on a computer (at most 900 × 620 px, the game dimmed behind) and the whole screen on a phone; each row is the setting's name on the left, its buttons on the right and the explanation underneath in quieter text (on upright phones, 560 px or narrower, the name goes above). It sits above the game's messages, and a click outside it closes it (only if the press started outside too). Tests pick a tab with `pauseTab(page, name)` from `tests/browser/lib.js`. The default graphics are the sharpest (`S.pixel` 1, `S.levels` 256); `S.gfx` = 2 marks settings that have had that default applied, so saved settings without it get it once. Pixel size Max (`S.pixel` 0) draws one game pixel per real screen pixel (`screenDpr()`: the device pixel ratio, at most 3), so a screen scaled above 100% or a phone gets finer detail than 1×, and at 100% it's the same as 1×; `watchDpr()` redraws when the window moves to another screen or the page is zoomed, and `pixNote()` says what Max gives on this screen. `S.cloth`, `S.cue` and `S.glove` are the looks in use ('none' for no gloves); `S.fxOff` lists the collection effects switched off; what's owned is in the locker (`owns()` checks). `S.spinOpen` (see Spin, fine aim and fine power). `S.volume` (0 to 1, 0.75 being the original loudness) replaced the old `S.sound` switch, which is carried over on load. `S.vibrate` buzzes for the local shooter's pots and fouls, only on touch devices with `navigator.vibrate`. The fullscreen buttons and the sideways hint are set up in the "phone and screen comforts" section of `game.js`. In the menus, the money counter (see Looks), the ideas lightbulb (see Ideas), Settings and Fullscreen sit in the top-right corner (`#menuIcons`; the icons are pixel art drawn by `setIcon()` from `ICONS`). `#menuIcons` comes after `#menu` and `#reel` in the page, so CSS hides it with the menu (`#menu[hidden] ~ #menuIcons`) and, while the reel is open, lifts it above the reel with the icons hidden and nothing tappable. On upright phones (560 px wide or less, over 500 px tall) the menu panel starts below the corner buttons, so a tall panel (the locker's lists) never runs under them; on phones held sideways they sit in the panel's top-right corner, beside the title. The footer hides when a screen has no main button; in a game they stay in the HUD.
- **Dev mode** (for the owner, to try every look): typing the dev word as the name on the Online screen switches it on or off (`isDevWord()`, `setDev()`). Only a SHA-256 fingerprint of `'retroRack:' + word` is in the code (`DEVW.print`), because the repository is public; never write the word into the repository. While it's on (`DEV`, localStorage `retroRack.dev`), `lockKey()` points the locker at a separate test locker (`retroRack.lockerDev`, made by `devLocker()`: every look, £100,000 and 50 of each case), Settings shows a Dev tab (Switch off; "Next case mythic": `DEV_MYTHIC`, which makes the next case opened give a mythic through `openCase()`'s `force`, then switches itself off; and "Empty test locker", `#sDevEmpty`: a fresh test locker with no looks and no rewards, £100,000 and 50 of each case, so the rules that depend on what's owned (a Treat case always new, mythics never repeated, collections paying out) behave as they do for a new player, which they can't when everything is owned; `devEmpty()`, localStorage `retroRack.devEmpty`, kept through reloads until "Fill test locker" gives it everything again or dev mode goes off; `devLocker()` leaves an empty one's looks and rewards alone) and the version line says DEV; switching off brings the real locker back and `checkLooks()` takes off anything not really owned. Career prize money earned meanwhile goes into the test locker.
- **Browser storage:** localStorage keys `retroRack.settings` (`S`), `retroRack.menu` (`M`, the menu choices and `M.last`), `retroRack.name` (online name), `retroRack.layouts` (saved trick-shot layouts), `retroRack.rotateHint` (the sideways hint dismissed) `retroRack.rejoin` (see Online play), `retroRack.career` (see Career), `retroRack.locker` (see Looks), `retroRack.dev`, `retroRack.lockerDev` and `retroRack.devEmpty` (dev mode), and `retroRack.devWord` and `retroRack.ideasSeen` (see Ideas). sessionStorage, per tab: `rr.cid` (online client id) and `rr.live`. Wrap every access in try/catch: storage can be missing or blocked.
- **Menu screens:** `#menu` holds a stack of `.mScreen` sections (home, single, multi, game, setup, practice, online, locker, and the career's cnew, career and cevent). `menuGo()`, `menuBack()` and `menuReset()` move between them; `menuSlide()` makes the whole panel glide across the screen (a copy of the old panel slides off one side as the real one arrives from the other; scope any `.mScreen` lookups to `#menuPanel` so the copy is left alone). `NAV` keeps the stack. While away from home one history entry is kept armed, so a phone's back gesture goes back a screen, cancels the waiting room, or opens Pause in a game, instead of leaving the page. `menuTable()` switches the table behind the menu to match the choice. `M.last` holds the setup for "Play again". Card pictures are drawn by `drawArt()`: arcade-style people sprites (`SPRITES`) and pixel balls. Every menu screen must fit a phone on its side (762×341) without scrolling. The shared styles `.row`, `.lbl`, `.seg`, `.note`, `.swatches` and `.actions` are also used by Settings and the overlays, so keep them when changing the menu CSS.
- **Phone layout:** the media queries in `shell.html` switch to it on narrow (760 px or less) or short (500 px or less) screens, so phones on their side get it too. `hudVars()` keeps four CSS variables current: `--bb` (bottom of the scoreboard), `--tb` (top of the bottom-left button grid, which the practice buttons and the chat menu stack above), and `--lw` and `--sw` (the right edge of the left-hand buttons or the trick panel, and the width of the spin panel, so the ball picker can sit between them on a phone on its side). Check HUD changes at phone-landscape sizes such as 762×341 and 915×412 as well as on a computer. In the phone layout the game's buttons (`.ib`: the left-hand tools, the practice and trick-shot buttons, the aim arrows, the replay bar) are 34 px squares showing a pixel icon from `ICONS` (`setIcon()`), their words hidden; on a computer the words show and the icons hide. `setLab()` changes a button's words, icon and `aria-label` together. A long press on one (`LP`, not the aim arrows, which turn while held) shows its name in `#ibTip` and swallows the click. Shoot keeps its word. `phoneUI` is the same media query in script.
- **Spin, fine aim and fine power:** the small ball beside the power bar (`#bSpin`, drawn by `drawMini()`) shows where the tip will strike and shows or hides the spin diagram (`#shot.noSpin`, `applySpinOpen()`): `S.spinOpen`, unset until first tapped, so phones start with it hidden and computers with it shown. Aim: ←/→ or the arrows turn `AIM_STEP` = 0.05° a press (Ctrl + ←/→ `AIM_FINE` = 0.01°; Ctrl with A or D is left to the browser); a held arrow button turns from 0.5°/s, doubling every 0.4 s up to 40°/s; sliding along the aim readout (`#aimTxt`, `jog`) turns `JOG` = 0.0025° per screen pixel, its tick marks moving with the aim. Power: the readout (`#powTxt`) shows the cue ball's speed off the tip to 0.01 m/s (`ballSpeed()`; spin takes a little of it); a held ↑/↓ or W/S changes the power smoothly (slowly with Shift); Ctrl + ↑/↓ moves the readout exactly `POW_FINE` = 0.01 m/s a press from what it shows (`stepSpeed()`, `setBallSpeed()`, which works the power back from a speed with `cueSpeedToPower()`), holding it doing nothing more (those presses never go into `keys`, so the held change doesn't run; Ctrl with W or S is left to the browser: Ctrl + W closes the tab); sliding along the readout (`pjog`) changes it `POW_JOG` = 0.0025 m/s per screen pixel, right for more, its tick marks moving with the speed.
- **Controls panel** (`#help`): single key presses go through `keyAct()`, which the panel's keys (`kbd.tap`, `tapKey()`) call too, so tapping a key does what pressing it does; power keys step by 0.025 per tap and Q/E turn the camera about 25°; the arrows written with Ctrl (inside `[data-ctrl]`) do the Ctrl step (0.01° or 0.01 m/s). Keys matching `KEY_STAY` (aim, power, spin) leave the panel open; the rest close it first. Shift, Ctrl and Alt aren't tappable.
- **Following the next shot** (`followShot()`, called by `endShot()` when the next turn is a local human's, state `aim`): in the free view (or the shot camera on its way back to it), `cam.free` becomes a view from just behind the cue ball along the line to the ball the cue points at (`nearestTarget()`, the one `aimAtNearest()` aims at), centred between them, at the player's own tilt (kept between 0.3 and 1.2 rad), the distance (at least 0.9 m) grown until the cue ball is within 70% of the lower half of the view and that ball within 55% of the upper half, clear of the scoreboard; the camera then glides there as usual (with reduced motion, `cam.cur` jumps). Cue view already follows the cue ball and the overhead view shows everything, so both are left alone; not on the break, after a resync, or for the computer's or an online opponent's turn. Switch: `S.follow` (Settings, "Follow next shot", on by default). Display only: nothing is sent online.
- **Ideas** (the ideas section of `game.js`, and `Ideas` in the relay): the lightbulb in the menus' corner (`#bIdea`, `openIdea()`) opens a box (`#idea`) for an idea of up to `IDEA_MAX` = 100 characters, sent by `sendIdea()` with the online name (`ideaName()`: what's in the Online screen's name box) and `BUILD`; Enter sends, Esc, the back gesture or a tap outside closes it. `ideaPost()` posts to the relay's `/ideas` (`ideaUrl()`, from `relayBase()`), and the lightbulb hides when there's no relay. In dev mode the lightbulb is the owner's inbox (`#ideas`, `openIdeas()`, `renderIdeas()`): `loadIdeas()` fetches them with the dev word, which the page keeps (`retroRack.devWord`) from when the word switched dev mode on (`setDev(on, word)`), or from the inbox's own box (`unlockIdeas()`, checked with `isDevWord()` first); switching dev mode off forgets it. The ideas come newest first with the name, date and version; those newer than `retroRack.ideasSeen` (`markRead()`, the newest one read) are marked, counted on the lightbulb (`ideaBadge()`) and announced by a note under the corner icons (`#ideaPop`, `showPop()`: gone after 12 s or at the next tap anywhere). Delete takes one off, Delete all empties it. Nothing is polled: one request at start-up in dev mode, and when the inbox opens or Refresh is pressed. Text is shown with `textContent`, never as markup.
- **Markers:** `markedBalls()` picks the balls the shooter is on (their group, then the black; the lowest ball in 9-ball; nothing on the break, an open table or in practice). `updateMarkers()` draws a camera-facing halo (a sprite) round each one, only while a local human is aiming. Display only: nothing is sent online. Switch: `S.markers`.
- **Shot camera:** a local human's Shoot (`shoot()`: the Shoot button, Space or Enter, and the trick shots' Show me) holds the aim for `SET_T` = 0.9 s (state `set`) while the camera's `'shot'` mode glides in, eased all the way (`cam.shot.from`), to the shooter's eyes: standing over the cue, about 40 cm up and 60 cm back, looking down the line from where the shot was played; then `beginStroke()` plays the stroke as usual (online, that's when the shot is sent, so both screens stay in step). `RISE_WAIT` = 0.3 s after the strike (state `moving`), the view rises over `RISE_T` = 1.2 s, eased, to `cam.high()`: above the long side you're nearer, `HIGH_PITCH` = 60° down, far enough back that the table and rails fill about 90% of the screen (or to `cam.top()` if you shot from the overhead view); meanwhile the spin and power panel and the buttons on the left fade (`.away` on `#shot`, `#tools` and `#practice`; they still work). Once the balls stop, `cam.shotOver()` puts back the view you had. Moving the camera mid-shot (a drag, Cue view or Overhead) keeps your view instead (and the buttons come back: the fade follows only the risen shot camera). Not for the computer's or an online opponent's shots; with reduced motion or the Settings switch off (`S.shotCam`, on by default) the stroke starts at once with no swing. If the shot can't go after the hold (the opponent's connection dropped, say), the view goes back. Display only: nothing new is sent online.

### Online play
- The browser in each seat runs the full game; the relay only forwards messages.
- **Messages sent by the relay:** `welcome` (with the seat), `peer` (on or off) and `full`.
- **Messages between players:** `hello` (name, whether a game is under way, `v` the version, and the player's `cue`, `cloth` and `glove`), `look` (a player changed their cue, cloth or gloves mid-game), `setup` (the host's rack, mode, aim guide, match length and `blackOne`, the "one visit on the black" rule), `state` (a full snapshot for resyncs), `aim` (live cue preview), `shot` (shot number, inputs, initial velocities and cue placement), `sync` (the shooter's table after the shot), `chat` (a preset index only), `concede`, `rerackOffer`, `rerackYes`, `rerackNo`, `again` and `bye`.
- **Looks:** each player's cue and gloves are shown on both screens, and the table wears the host's (seat 0's) cloth: `takeLooks()` reads them from `hello` and `look`, accepting only known design ids (anything else becomes the house cue, no gloves, or the receiver's own cloth). `NET.cloth`, `NET.peerCue` and `NET.peerGlove` are cleared on leaving. Display only: nothing here affects the physics.
- **The input-only check:** the receiver validates cue ball placement and recomputes the strike itself. The shooter's `sync` is then a cross-check; a mismatch shows a warning and adopts the shooter's table.
- **Reconnecting:** uses a per-tab client id kept in sessionStorage, so a reload keeps the seat. A duplicated tab copies sessionStorage, so each open page marks the id as in use (`rr.live`, cleared on `pagehide`); a copy that finds the mark picks a fresh id.
- **Rejoin:** whenever an online game starts, is restored or a shot ends, the page saves the room code, its client id, the opponent's name and the mode in localStorage (`retroRack.rejoin`: a list, one entry per client id, kept 3 hours). The home screen's Rejoin button (`updateRejoin()`) puts that id back into sessionStorage and joins the room, so the relay returns the same seat and an opponent whose page is still open sends the table (`state`). If neither page is still open, a fresh frame starts. Quit, Menu on the result screen, Cancel in the waiting room, a full room and the opponent's `bye` (leaving on purpose) forget the entry. Each page in a room holds a Web Lock named `retroRack.seat.<cid>` (`holdSeat()`, freed by `leaveOnline()` or by closing the page), and the button skips entries whose lock is held, so a second tab on the same device never takes over a seat that's still open.
- **Versions:** `tools/build.js` stamps `BUILD` in `game.js` with a fingerprint of the game code, shown at the bottom of the menu. `hello` carries it. Before a game starts, a mismatch (or a peer sending no version, i.e. older than this check) stops the game starting and tells the players to reload. Mid-game it only warns, since the `sync` cross-check keeps the tables matched.
- **Installable app:** `game.js` adds the manifest link and registers `sw.js` only on http(s), so a copy opened from disk stays quiet. Service workers don't run from `file://`, so test the app parts through a local web server.
- **Relay (`relay/src/index.js`):** a `Room` Durable Object per room code (seats, reconnects, listing), plus a single `Lobby` Durable Object for the open-rooms list that menus watch over a WebSocket. A single `Ideas` Durable Object keeps the players' ideas: `POST /ideas` with a plain-text JSON body (so there's no CORS preflight; the answers carry `access-control-allow-origin: *`), `op` `send` (`text`, `name`, `v`) for anyone, and `list`, `delete` (`id`) and `clear` only with `key`, the dev word, whose fingerprint must match `DEV_PRINT` (keep it the same as `DEVW.print` in `game.js`; the `ideas` browser test checks; `env.DEV_PRINT` overrides it, for the tests' stand-in word). Because anyone can post without the game, the relay does the checking: control and invisible characters taken out, line breaks made spaces, 100 characters at most, a name of 14 without `<` or `>`, `IDEA_DAY` = 5 a day from one address (kept only as a fingerprint, in `rate`, forgotten the next day), `IDEAS_DAY` = 300 a day in all, and the newest `IDEAS_KEEP` = 500 kept. Refusals answer 200 with `{ ok: false, why }` (`long`, `empty`, `limit`, `busy`, `key`, `bad`), so the game's console stays clean. Migrations are `v1` (Room), `v2` (Lobby) and `v3` (Ideas). Adding a Durable Object class needs a **new** migration tag; never edit existing ones.
- **Compatibility:** changing the message format means both players need the new version. Keep old fields working where possible.

## Testing

1. **Physics, rules, career and looks:** `node tests/physics.test.js`, `node tests/career.test.js` and `node tests/looks.test.js` must each report 0 failed. It covers bit-identical results, pockets on both tables, breaks, the rules of every mode, the trick-shot demos, and that `CONTEXT.md` and `CONTEXT-SHORT.md` match the code and notes (if not, run the build).
2. **Browser tests:** `node tests/browser/run-all.js` builds the game, serves three copies of it locally like GitHub Pages (the current build, an old build from before the version check, and the current build with a different version number), starts the relay locally with Wrangler, then runs every `*.test.js` in `tests/browser/` in headless Chromium and prints a summary. Each check prints `PASS` or `FAIL`, and each file also fails if anything reaches the browser console.
   - Needs Playwright with Chromium (preinstalled in Claude Code's cloud sessions; elsewhere `npm install --no-save playwright` then `npx playwright install chromium`) and npm, which installs the relay's packages once into a temporary folder. Nothing is written into the repository; screenshots go to the temporary folder, printed at the end.
   - Software rendering is slow: the whole set takes about 12 minutes in a Claude Code cloud session. To keep it so, `lib.js` starts test pages at pixel size 3× and 8 shades rather than the game's sharper defaults, unless a test sets its own settings or opens the page with `?realdefaults` (as the check of the defaults does). For a small change, run the files that cover it, e.g. `node tests/browser/run-all.js menus fit`.
   - The files: `menus` (every menu route, back navigation, Play again), `fit` (every menu screen fits a phone on its side without scrolling, with no buttons or words under the corner buttons), `online` (games through the relay, reload rejoin, duplicated tabs, version mismatches), `rejoin` (the Rejoin button), `comforts` (volume, fullscreen, sideways hint, vibration, pixel size Max at three screen scales; over http), `markers` (the halos on the balls you're on; over http), `halloween` (the Halloween tab in season, Treat cases always new, the day's Trick case, out of season, all thirty paying their rewards once, the Haunted Hall and its switch, Halloween looks moving at the table, the tab fitting a phone; fixed dates, over http), `phone` (icon squares on a phone and words on a computer, a long press showing a name, the spin diagram's toggle and its kept choice, sliding on the aim readout, the gentle hold, Ctrl + arrow, fine power with Ctrl + ↑/↓ and by sliding on the power readout, the Controls panel's tappable keys), `shotcam` (the shot camera: the held aim with no fading of the buttons, then the stroke, the rise to the whole table and the faded buttons, from the overhead view too, your shots only, back to your view and the buttons, its switch, reduced motion), `follow` (after a pot the free view goes behind the cue ball to the next ball, both in view below the scoreboard, also after moving the view mid-shot; Cue view and overhead left alone, its switch, only your turns against the computer, reduced motion; on a phone on its side), `hud` (no HUD pieces overlap at phone, tablet and computer sizes), `pause` (Pause and Settings: the tabs, Match first in a game with what's being played, Resume from any tab, the highlight and the sliding pages, ← and →, the last tab remembered from the menus, a large panel on a computer and the whole screen on a phone with pages that scroll under the tabs, the tab row on an upright phone, the Dev tab, reduced motion), `file` (the page opened from disk), `app` (installable app, offline start, updates), `add-balls-and-black` (Add balls in the trick-shot editor, and "one visit on the black"), `career` (a new career, draws, playing, leaving and resuming a match, winning and losing, money, unlocking, save to file and load, withdrawing, retiring; served over http, like `locker`), `ideas` (players' ideas: the lightbulb and the box, 100 characters, Enter, Esc and the back gesture, the relay's cleaning and its five a day, the owner's inbox in dev mode with a stand-in word, the note and the number of new ones, Delete and Delete all, dev mode off forgetting the word, unlocking with the word, and that the relay and the game check the same fingerprint; over http, against the local relay), `money` (the money counter: in the corner of every menu screen, counting down and up with the change beside it, holding while the reel spins so a duplicate's sale shows only when it stops, money earned in a game counted in on the way back, another open page, hidden in a game and in Preview, tapping it opens the locker, dev mode's mark, reduced motion; and the Diamond case's card; over http), `shop` (tier tabs, the shop in the locker, bought looks in Settings and in every game, venues), `looks` (online: each player's cue and gloves and the host's cloth on both screens, the day's first online win), `locker` (old saves moving in, opening a case and the reel, buying, duplicates, two open pages, earning, tiles in their rarity's colour, the show behind the reel for a common and a mythic, and still with reduced motion, Preview with looks tried on (and leaving it by a tap, the back gesture and Esc), the shop's collection rows, completing a collection (the reel's message, the reward paid once, every effect on and its switch), the inventory in rarity order, patterned cloths, gloves and moving mythics at the table, each glove one low-poly piece on its skeleton with the cue hand wrapped round the cue, the robot's parts and steel, a glove's reaction to a foul, dev mode with a stand-in word and its "next case mythic" switch, the empty test locker (Treat cases always new, forced mythics never repeated, kept through a reload, filled again, forgotten when dev mode goes off); served over http, because pages opened from disk don't reliably share storage changes).
   - The test sites' `config.js` points at the local relay, never the real one. `run-all.js` also starts the local relay fresh each time (its rooms, ideas and daily limits cleared) and gives it the fingerprint of the stand-in dev word `testword` (`.dev.vars`, `DEV_PRINT`), so the tests can read ideas without the real word. A relay you started yourself is used as it is, and the `ideas` test then says so.
   - Test pages see a fixed date, 15 June 2026, outside every season, so what a frame earns doesn't depend on the calendar; a context can ask for its own with `newContext({ testDate: 'YYYY-MM-DD' })` (`fixDate()` in `lib.js`; the clock still runs).
   - A test that reloads a page, reopens one or uses two pages needs storage to carry over, so it runs over http (`SITE.new`): in the test browser, pages opened from disk don't always share or keep their storage.
   - New features get a new test file, or new checks in the file that fits. Keep test windows small (about 480×360) unless the size is what's being tested.
3. **By eye:** for gameplay or UI changes, open `index.html` in a browser and try the change, watching the console. For anything online, run the relay locally (see Commands) and play yourself in two separate browser windows.

## Keeping the notes current

Update this file, `HANDOVER.md` and `README.md` in the same change as the code, then rebuild so the context packs follow. In `HANDOVER.md`, add the change to the list of changes shipped once it is merged. Notes go stale quietly, so when you find one that no longer matches the code, fix it.

## Ideas list

The full list, with what has been built and what was set aside, is in `HANDOVER.md` (section 7). Next candidates:
- Career Stage 3: rivals and short story cards, trick-shot challenges, a trophy cabinet, and the world final after the national tour.
- Stats (pot percentage, wins per CPU level, longest run, break-and-dish), stored in the browser.
- Keeping the table when both online players leave, so Rejoin can restore it (each page would save its snapshot; needs a protocol change, so both players need the new version).
- More trick-shot layouts. Use `tools/find-trick-demos.js` to give each one a working demo.
- A colour-blind-friendly marking option for reds and yellows.
- A progression pass: the owner wants one, but not yet.

---

# File: HANDOVER.md

# Retro Rack: decisions, history and changes

This file records why Retro Rack is the way it is: the decisions and their reasons, reference numbers, the ideas list, and a dated list of every change shipped. It began as the handover from the Claude chat where the game was first built; work has continued in Claude Code since. Read it together with `AGENTS.md`, which explains how the code works and how to change it.

---

## 1. Current status

- The game is live at https://flopp-r.github.io/retro-rack/, served by GitHub Pages from the public repository `github.com/flopp-r/retro-rack` (branch `main`, root folder).
- The relay is a Cloudflare Worker named `retro-rack-relay` on the free plan, deployed automatically by Cloudflare Workers Builds from the repository's `relay/` folder. Its `workers.dev` address is in `config.js`; leave that file alone.
- Everything in section 9 (changes shipped) is live. The version shown at the bottom of the menu matches the `BUILD` value in the committed `index.html`.

---

## 2. Working preferences

Personal working preferences are kept in the owner's own Claude Code settings, not in this public repository. Project conventions (plain-language explanations, UK English, metric units, a clean console, testing before pushing) are in `AGENTS.md`.

---

## 3. How the project got here (short history)

1. **First version:** a single-file 3D pool game. Pixel-art rendering (low-resolution render target, depth outlines, Bayer dithering, colour quantisation) and realistic physics. 8-ball, 9-ball and practice modes, a CPU with four levels, a same-device mode, aim guides by difficulty, an orbit camera, a cue view and an overhead view.
2. **Clean console:** switched from the deprecated three.js UMD build to the r159 ES-module build, inlined. Migrated from `useLegacyLights` to physical light units, matching the old look (see section 5).
3. **Reds & yellows:** added a 7 ft British table with 2-inch balls and tight rounded pockets, a baulk line, and British pub rules.
4. **Aiming:** fine aim became one step per key press (0.05°, auto-repeat ignored). Shift held with the arrow keys turns smoothly, at 10°/s easing up to 40°/s. An aim angle readout was added.
5. **Spin widget:** fixed it so the label always takes two lines (no layout jumping), the dot tracks the pointer exactly, and it renders at an exact 3× pixel scale. The HUD was made about 15–20% smaller.
6. **Online 1v1:**
   - GitHub Pages plus the Cloudflare relay.
   - Deterministic physics.
   - The "input-only check" anti-cheat.
   - Live aim preview, reconnect and resync after a reload, and rematches.
   - The relay address moved into `config.js`.
7. **Open rooms list:** Listed or Private rooms, plus notifications stacked in one column so they never overlap.
8. **The update package:** instant replay, touch fine-aim buttons, quick chat, concede and re-rack, matches (first to 3, 5 or 7), trick shots with "Show me" demos plus a ball editor and saved layouts, and the project restructure for Claude Code (`src/`, `vendor/`, `tools/`, `tests/`).
9. **Claude Code (from 6 October 2026):** every change since has gone through a pull request; they are listed in section 9.

---

## 4. Key decisions and why

**Delivery**
- **One self-contained `index.html`,** with fonts and three.js inlined, so the game works offline as a single downloaded file. Online play needs `config.js` next to it.
- **three.js is pinned at r159** (ES-module build, wrapped by `tools/build.js`). Upgrading is possible but not needed. If you do upgrade, re-check lighting, the `DepthTexture` outline pass and the console.

**Online play**
- **Determinism over server-side physics.** The relay never simulates. Both browsers run identical physics; the rules for that are in `AGENTS.md`. This keeps the relay tiny and free, and enables replays.
- **The trust model is aimed at friendly games:**
  - Receivers accept only shot inputs and recompute the result themselves. Cue ball placement is validated, and the strike velocities are checked.
  - The shooter's `sync` is a cross-check; on a mismatch, the receiver warns and adopts the shooter's table.
  - Known gaps: aiming aids on someone's own screen can't be detected. A modified client could stall a game by sending an illegal cue ball placement (honest clients can't do this). (A duplicated browser tab used to share the original tab's client id and fight over the seat; it now picks a fresh id.)

**Rooms**
- A listed room appears only while its creator waits, and disappears when a second player joins or the creator leaves. Nobody can join a game in progress or take a seat someone dropped out of.
- The list forgets rooms after 45 minutes waiting. Unused rooms are deleted after 6 hours.
- Rejoin is kept on the player's own device for 3 hours, well inside the 6 hours a room lasts. It uses the same seat-by-client-id reconnect as a reload, so the relay needed no change. The table itself lives only in the players' open pages, so if both close, the frame starts again.
- Chat is preset messages only (sent as an index), so there's nothing to moderate in listed rooms.

**Free tier**
- Uses the WebSocket Hibernation API, keep-alive pings answered automatically, no polling, and the open rooms list is pushed over WebSockets. Usage is far inside the free limits.

**Tooling**
- **Changes reach GitHub through Claude Code's GitHub connector.** This has been working well; keep using it. Pushing to `main` deploys to the live site, and to the relay if `relay/` changed.

**Game rules**
- **8-ball:** WPA-style but no called pockets. An illegal break (no pot and fewer than 4 balls to a rail) counts as a foul. The 8 potted on the break is re-spotted.
- **9-ball:** no push-out rule.
- **Reds & yellows:**
  - A foul gives the opponent two visits, and the first is a free ball (any ball may be hit first).
  - The two-visit advantage carries while the player keeps potting.
  - An in-off gives ball in hand behind the baulk line.
  - Potting the opponent's colour is a foul.
  - The black on the break is re-spotted, not re-racked.
  - "Lose the second visit when on the black" is an option on the setup screen ("On black: Two visits / One visit"), off by default because pubs differ.
- **Physics limitation:** the cue is always level, so there are no jump or massé shots.

**Trick shots**
- Each demo was found by searching with the real physics (`tools/find-trick-demos.js`) and checked for robustness, meaning nearby aims also work.

**Career (chosen by the owner, October 2026)**
- **A tour of tournaments,** tier by tier from the pub to a world final, rather than league seasons or a simple ladder. Each event is an 8-player knockout against named computer players, with longer matches in later rounds.
- **All three game types across the tiers:** reds & yellows in the pubs, 8-ball in clubs and halls, 9-ball at the top.
- **Rewards are looks only:** prize money will buy cloths, cues, ball sets and venue looks. No entry fees, so you can't go broke. Player "upgrades" were rejected: your skill is your real aim, and boosting it would fight the physics.
- **The aim guide is chosen when a career starts** and stays fixed for it, like a difficulty setting.
- **Opponents' strength is measured, not guessed:** `tools/sim-career.js` plays each against the Medium CPU (80 frames, accurate to about ±45 rating points). Personalities come from the CPU's own settings: accuracy, long-pot weakness, safety play, break power, and nerves on the final ball. The pub tier runs from about 1245 to 1560 (Medium is 1500), so its final boss is a little stronger than the Medium CPU; later tiers will go much higher.
- **Matches between computer players** in a draw are settled from their ratings with a seeded random number, so they're instant and never change on reloading.
- **Saving:** in the browser (localStorage) after every shot, plus Save to file / Load from file. There are no accounts or cloud saves (that would need a login and server storage). Safari can clear a site's storage after 7 days without a visit, and clearing browsing data deletes it, so the README tells players to keep a file copy.
- **Built in stages,** each playable: Stage 1 (pub tier, opponents, draws, saving), Stage 2 (more tiers and game types, venue looks, spending prize money), Stage 3 (rivals, story cards, trick-shot challenges, trophy cabinet, world final).

**Career, Stage 2 (October 2026)**
- **Difficulty aimed at the owner,** who beats the Medium CPU and loses to Hard: the national tour's best (The Metronome) measures about 70 points below the Hard CPU in 9-ball, and every tier's championship has the tier's strongest player. Yardsticks measured with `tools/sim-career.js`, Medium = 1500: Easy 1245 (8-ball) and 1309 (9-ball); Hard 1819 (8-ball) and 1728 (9-ball). 9-ball squeezes ratings together (its breaks and lucky pots matter more), so for the stars a 9-ball rating is stretched by 319/228 to compare with 8-ball. The tiers: pub 1245 to 1560, club 1420 to 1625, hall 1515 to 1705 (8-ball), national 1535 to 1655 (9-ball, 1549 to 1717 stretched).
- **Personalities can cost more than expected.** Nerves on the final ball hurt far more in 9-ball, where most frames are decided on the 9: The Metronome with nerves 1.5 measured only 1535, so his nerves are now 1.25.
- **The shop sells cloths and cues only,** so the balls always stay readable; cloth colours were chosen to keep reds, yellows and the black clear.
- **Bought looks belong to the device,** not the career: they work in every game, survive retiring, and come with a career loaded from a file.
- **Online, looks are shared:** each player's cue shows on both screens, and the table wears the host's cloth (one table, one cloth, like the host choosing the game). Unknown ids from the other side fall back to the defaults.
- **A room per tier:** carpet, walls, skirting and the neon sign (with the event's name) change; the table and lighting don't.

**Settings and graphics (October 2026)**
- **The sharpest picture is the default** (pixel size 1×, full colours), as the owner chose, and it was applied once to everyone already playing (`S.gfx`), since saved settings would otherwise keep the old 3× and 8 shades. It costs more on phones and softens the pixel-art look; anyone can pick a bigger pixel size in Settings.
- **Pixel size Max, not a literal 0.5×** (the owner asked for finer aim lines). 1× already uses every pixel of a screen at 100%, so finer than that only helps where the screen has more pixels than the page: Windows scaled to 125% or more, and phones. Max matches the screen exactly (one game pixel per screen pixel). A literal 0.5× was tried: it matched Max at 200%, was a little softer at 150%, and at 100% the browser shrank it, leaving the 1-pixel aim line thin, faint and dotted. Max costs more drawing in proportion (2.25 times the pixels at 150%), so it isn't the default.
- **Settings is a big panel** in four sections, two columns on computers, with bigger buttons; it fits a 1280×720 screen without scrolling. A click outside it closes it. (Replaced in October 2026 by the tabbed panel below.)
- **Pause and Settings got tabs (October 2026, the first step of a menu overhaul; the owner found the panel messy and wanted clean, sophisticated menus with clean animations):** one long two-column page became tabs along the top, each its own page that scrolls. In a game it opens on Match (Resume, Re-rack, Concede, Quit as big buttons, with the score), because that's what Pause is for; the settings follow as Play, Looks, Display and Sound (Display and Sound were kept apart rather than merged, so no tab is long), with Dev only in dev mode, its three switches each given a row. A Resume button at the end of the tab row was chosen over a bar of game buttons under every tab, which would have taken about a seventh of a phone's height from the settings. A large panel on a computer (a single column needs no more than 900 px) and the whole screen on a phone. The motion is the pattern for the rest of the menus: a highlight that glides under the chosen tab and the page sliding in from that side over 0.22 s, the panel fading and growing slightly as it opens, all left out with reduced motion. From the menus it reopens on the last tab used (not saved), so changing the volume twice doesn't mean finding Sound twice; in a game it always opens on Match.
- **The browser tests draw in software,** so the sharper default made them slower.

**Looks: cases, the locker and gloves (October 2026)**
- **Earning, as the owner chose:** cases from career event wins (a better grade for championships), from frames won against the computer (a better grade on harder levels), from the day's first online win, and bought with money; every case costs money to open. Duplicates are sold for money (the owner chose this over a crafting currency). Practice and same-device games earn nothing, because they're too easy to farm.
- **One wallet per device, in the locker:** opening cases needs money outside the career too, so the career's prize money, the pay for frames won against the computer, the daily online win and sold duplicates all go into one locker. It survives retiring a career and travels inside a career file. Loading a file merges its locker in and never takes anything away (re-loading the same file can't stack money).
- **Fair randomness:** the odds are shown on every case, an epic or better is guaranteed within 8 cases, and legendaries only come in silver cases and better. The reel decides the prize first and then lands on it honestly: the other items on the strip are drawn from the case's own odds, and where it stops within the prize's tile is random, so it never fakes a near miss (the trick real-money loot boxes use).
- **A starter case:** a new locker has one silver case and £40 to open it, so everyone sees the reel straight away.
- **Mythic, a fifth rarity, in every case (the owner chose "every case, tiny"):** 0.2% in bronze, 0.5% silver, 1% gold, 3% diamond (6% since diamond cases became epic or better, below), so even a bronze case can hold one. It counts for the guarantee like any epic or better. Six mythics, two of each kind (the owner's pick), and each one moves, which nothing else does.
- **Reel tiles in their rarity's colour (the owner asked, to tell rarities apart at a glance):** a fade from a dark shade at the top to nearly the full colour at the bottom was chosen over solid colour, which washed out pale pictures on pale common and gold tiles.
- **Preview at the table, rather than a model in the locker (the owner's pick):** it shows the real cue, gloves and cloth exactly as in a game, animations and all, and costs no room on a phone's locker screen. The camera swings round the side of the cue instead of circling right round, because from behind the butt end the cue hand filled the screen. Looks you don't own can be tried on too (the owner's pick), so you can see a look before buying it or chasing it in cases.
- **The case opening is a show (the owner asked for a dimmed room, a reel filling most of the screen and a background in the prize's colours, livelier the rarer it is):** the room stays barely visible behind the dim rather than going black, so it still feels like the same room. Nothing flashes: brightness changes smoothly, no faster than about 3 times a second, and with reduced motion the reel doesn't spin, the screen doesn't shake and the background is one still picture.
- **Gloves look like the sports and tactical gloves in shooting games (the owner asked for "CS gloves"),** after three rounds of prototypes: blocky cartoon gloves, then smooth realistic hands, then simplified ones. The owner asked for less wrist, less surface detail and no fingertips, so each hand is a rounded palm, two jointed sections per finger and thumb ending in a stub, and a short cuff. The glove types differ by small, low parts (a slim back panel, a knuckle bar or guards, finger pads, a strap tab, wraps), kept subtle at the owner's request. Most novelty gloves (oven mitt, washing-up, goalkeeper and so on) were replaced by real glove types; boxing, robot and skeleton stay as novelties. Gloves (and their reactions) add a little of their own colour as glow, because the faces towards the camera get little light and otherwise look grey. Reactions are off with reduced motion.
- **Low-poly gloves (October 2026; the owner found the jointed-parts hands clunky and their blocks of colour too noticeable, and asked for something closer to CS):** a first attempt sculpted a smooth, realistic hand (rounded shapes melted together and meshed, with shading in the creases); the owner found it "morphish" and the shadows ugly, and pointed to a low-poly hand on Sketchfab (which the cloud session's network couldn't reach, so it was matched by style, not copied). Each hand is now a low-poly mesh: flat faces, six-sided fingers, an eight-sided palm and cuff, no shading of its own, bent by a skeleton. Panels are whole faces, and every trim is toned towards the glove's main colour (accent 60%, cuff 75%, back panel 40% of the way), so they read as panels rather than raised blocks. The fingers have all three sections again: the earlier "no fingertips" choice made them stubby, which was part of the clunky look; this was put to the owner. The cuffs keep their earlier short lengths. Boxing is one faceted mitt; the robot claw and the skeleton stay as jointed parts, since they're meant to look mechanical and bony.
- **The owner's own hand model (October 2026):** the owner supplied a procedural hand module, "Retro Rack hands" (one low-poly skinned mesh per hand, 17 bones, poses with cue anchors), and asked for it to be built in. It was checked on this project's three.js (r159) first: it renders correctly, the grip wraps the cue and the bridge lies flat. It went in as a port rather than a separate file, because the game is one self-contained page and three.js is imported as a module rather than a global: the same sizes, rings, poses and anchors, with texture positions added for the patterned gloves, the glove types' extras (back panel, knuckle guard, finger pads, strap, nails) mapped onto its parts, its fifteen parts merged into eight materials (fewer draw calls), and three reaction poses added (fist, thumbs-up, open hand). Two things were found and handled: a higher cue line (top spin) would have lifted the bridge off the cloth, so the hand tips up on its fingertips instead; and full back spin lowers the cue about 1 cm into the index knuckle, visible only from in front of the hand, which was left as it is. While here, the boxing glove's bridge was moved so the cue no longer runs through the mitt, and a textured glove's knuckle guard now wears the texture instead of a flat bright block.
- **Shot camera (October 2026; the owner asked for each shot to feel real, from the player's eyes, and picked "swing in, then back" for their own shots only):** the view swings down behind the cue during the backswing (about half a second, so the shot isn't delayed and online play is untouched), stays where the shot was played while the balls roll, and goes back to your view once they stop. Following the ball, or staying in first person, were the other choices. Off with reduced motion, and a Settings switch for anyone who prefers a still camera. After trying it, the owner found the view too low and close and the swing rushed: it now stands higher and further back (about 40 cm up and 60 cm back), and pressing Shoot holds the aim for 0.9 s while the view glides in on an eased curve before the cue moves. Online, the shot is sent when the stroke starts, after the hold, so both screens stay in step; the message itself is unchanged. After the strike the view now rises to a high angle over the long side of the table (the owner asked to see everything once the shot is taken, and picked the high angle over a straight-down view, which reads like a diagram and hides the reactions, and over a view from behind the shooter, which turns the table sideways on some shots). It waits 0.3 s so the strike is seen from the player's eyes, then rises over 1.2 s.
